ParlaMint-ES-GA_2018-12-04-DSPG094.u266	Thank you, President. Good afternoon again, gentlemen. This evening, the Socialist Group brings this non-legislative proposal to be debated in the plenary after the replies given in the Commission 8th by the Director General for Fisheries Development of the Galician Union, which has left us extremely concerned, with statements such as the following: that nautical-fishing training is very expensive, that nautical-fishing training in the Navy is covered, although they recognize that it is difficult to access this training and that the courses held in the Navy are only five of them during this year, and only one of them to acquire basic competences. On the one hand, they recognize that access to this training is complicated, and on the other hand, they say that training in the Navy has been sufficient, and yet we have come to recognize that many people prefer to go to courses by paying or moving to other autonomous communities to carry them out. Do you think if you offer training in the Lugo Navy enough, the sea workers would prefer to move to other territories to form or pay in some academies to get this training? What is clear is that the sector is lacking in generational importance and training is fundamental. From the Galician Union they can't say they're going to continue studying needs, there must be immediate approaches. And in the budgets of the Union training in relative terms has not increased compared to the previous year. He doesn't have that weight in the budgets of the Union that the Council itself of Galicia says he has. Focusing on the proposal that we are debating, the fishing sector in the province of Lugo is one of the main economic motors of the region of the Navy, where two of Galicia's largest ports are located: Burela and Celero, not forgetting the rest of the ports of the region, such as Ribadeo, Foz, San Cibrao and O Vice-Vicdo. Burela and Celeiro not only stand out for their sales volumes; in 2017 they moved more than 127 million euros with fishing. The business volume of the fishing sector represents 26% of Galicia's total, and more than 21% of the far-away discharges in our autonomous community come from the Luminous ports, thus converting the Navy into a large fresh fishing line in Europe and thus ratifying the weight it has in the economy and society of the Navy, both for direct and indirect jobs. But Burela and Celero also stand out for their important fleet, in which we must emphasize the high number of large-scale vessels, many of them fishing in international and community waters, which implies the need for a greater number of nautical-fishing titits, as well as a higher level of training. Nearly 18 percent of the ships in the Navy are ready for large tonnage. The demand for trained personnel for the fisheries sector is increasing, as we can see in SEPE reports in Lugo. Within the occupations of difficult coverage, most of these sound from sea professionals. But it's not a matter of time-consuming necessity, as can be seen in the history of these reports. Covering the decks of ships' controls is difficult. It's hard to find captains, patterns and machine personnel. And it's also difficult to find and cover standards and machine posts, when they require more titration. Lately we're starting to get used to hearing that some ships couldn't leave our docks because of crew failure. In Vigo, by setting an example, more than a dozen cases in the past year, and in the Lugo Navy we don't escape this problem. A few weeks ago, a 30-foot longboat had to be moored to the port for several days in Burela because of a captain's failure. But there are many more cases. Cases of high-level patterns that work as sailors because they finish their studies in an official school but need to complement their training with advanced health courses, rescue and fires that are not taught in schools, and they have to go to private academies, paying more than 3,000 euros for this training. And a sailor who needs to renew basic training must also, on many occasions, turn to private centres because of the shortage of supply of this training. It should be recalled that educational activities are transferred to the Autonomous Community, in accordance with Royal Decree 523/2006 of 5 May. If we analyze the offer of training for bridge titles and machines in Galicia, we see that the centres where marine-fishing family cycles are provided are all in the provinces of Coruña and Pontevedra, there is none in the province of Lugo. Today morning the Counsellor acknowledged that training centres were full of people, and many of those people who are on waiting lists for these training centres are people from the Lugo Navy. Certificates of professionalism are the instrument of official accreditation of the professional qualifications of the national professional qualifications catalogue in the field of employment administration. They believe the set of professional competences that enable the development of an identifiable work in the productive system, without which this constitutes a regulation of the professional exercise. They are official and valid throughout the national territory and are issued by SEPE and the competent bodies of the autonomous communities. In addition, we must bear in mind that professionals at sea must have certificates of expertise, which are intended to complete or refine training on certain safety issues or to enable them to exercise certain functions or specialties on ships. These are courses that, once they are done, should also be updated periodically. The order of 31 July 2014 establishing the bases and regulating the procedure for obtaining the titles of seamen, fishermen, fishing pattern and multi-purpose coastal pattern of the autonomous community, in Article 2 thereof, establishes that the authorization, organization, monitoring, control and evaluation of courses and activities regulated in this order corresponds to the fisheries council, through the general management with competence in the field of nautical and maritime-fishing education. In addition, the resolution of 15 September 2016 of the Directorate General for Fisheries Development establishes the method of teleformation for obtaining the nautical-fishing professional qualification of the fishing mariner. But if we focus on the province of Lugo, and more specifically on the Marines, the situation in nautical-fishing is painful, since there is no centre providing vocational training for the nautical-fishing family, focusing — as I said — on the provinces of Coruña or Pontevedra or on the neighbouring autonomous communities. No certificates of professionalism are currently being issued; an old sector claim, which is to be obtained through professional experience. Many years ago, courses were being held in the Celery Fishing Delegation that have now disappeared, some of which are being held in private academies. In case of access to the teleformation offered by the Council of the Sea, they must travel to the provinces of Coruña and Pontevedra to perform the present examinations. The training offer, which can almost be defined as null in the region, requires even courses such as risk prevention courses outside our autonomous community, with the consequent economic cost and the aggravating lack of time for seafarers, especially those of high fishing, where they have to stay on land in a tide to be able to access those courses, without which they could not embark. But the shortage of training isn't only given to bosses and controls, but also to the marine level. We all know, and the sector demands that basic training courses be carried out in the area, and with some assiduity. This lack of training is also seen in the Navy with other collectives, such as the networks, who have been asking the Council of the Sea for training in that area to obtain the certificate of professionalism, since there were great demands and could be discharged in the social security system in order to exercise this profession. In the middle of this year, Depute de Lugo had to ensure free training for 15 red tapes to obtain the certificate of professionalism, thus complementing the training received in 2016. [[Applause]] Well, despite the importance of the fishing sector in the province and the need for sea-fishing personnel to cover the marine fleet's demand and ensure the generational relief, the training supply is not only insufficient, but it is aggravating compared to the rest of Galician coastal provinces. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2018-12-04-DSPG094.u284	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, very late for everyone. Given the hours that are and the tiredness that we all have, speaking now about the CAP and about the articulate can be a little tedious. But well, I'll try to be as concise as possible. In August this same year, the former rural adviser, Mrs Vázquez, signed with some agricultural organizations — not with all — and with some cooperatives a document reflecting the Government's institutional position on reforming the common agricultural policy. The document was delivered to the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food for Galician arrivals to be present in Spain as a Member State of the European Union. The text called for a single strategic plan by state, for the aid of the CAP, which would collect the singularities of each autonomous community. So the Union fixed the position of the Autonomous Community in view of the future common agricultural policy, the object of which it had been the guarantor and consequence of a profitable, sustainable and innovative regional agricultural sector, which had fixed the population in rural areas. At the first point of this document it was literally said that the committee maintained the proposal that each country could design a model of its own application, drawing up a national strategic plan, giving Member States more capacity to adapt the CAP to its territorial, productive and sectoral reality. Later on, this document added that we considered it essential that, as the draft regulation establishes, there should be a single Strategic Plan of the CAP by Member State. The new regulation should establish uniform criteria for all States. The Galician Union opted to demand this only strategic plan, in line with what the former Central Government had already done, as well as the current Central Government. This, to some extent, contradicted the desire expressed by the Rural Environment to meet the differences and peculiarities of each region or autonomous community. The Common Agricultural Policy, more known as the CAP, was a tool created in 1962. It has been one of the fundamental policies and is an essential element in the institutional system within the European Economic Community for two strategic sectors such as agriculture and livestock. Its objectives are set out in Article 39 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and are as follows: increasing agricultural productivity — today in Galicia is not being met and, more importantly, it is a falling sector — ensuring a fair standard of living throughout agricultural production — today in Galicia is not being met — and stabilising markets — it is not being met either in Galicia or in the state as a whole —; ensuring security of supplies and ensuring consumer supply at reasonable prices and quality food — not being met either. None of these objectives are being met today. In 2017 the European Commission sent the communication to make the legislative proposals. The Rural Environment Council, together with some agricultural and cooperative organisations, has prepared this document, which is currently under discussion in Brussels. The measures contained in this document were signed by the Unions Agrarians, by Asaga, by the Galician Association of Agri-Food Cooperatives - Agaca - or by the CLUN Cooperative. The agreement was transferred to the Ministry of Agriculture to incorporate this joint position from all over the Spanish territory on the reform of the Common Agricultural Policy. In the German agricultural sector, an agreement was reached on the following bases, second, in addition, the Council explained both in a statement and in the Council's question of En Marea made to it 15 days ago: to retain funding exclusively from European funds for direct aid to the CAP in order to prevent differences between producers depending on the support of each Member State, in favour of the Government of Greece that there is a single plan per State but — there was the advice — that the internal characteristics of agricultural production and raw materials are recognized — and, in some respects, they are giving us some reason to this proposal of law that we bring — to make a maximum level of aid for the exploitation of EUR 100,000; to regulate measures that allow for faster response to price swings of agricultural products and raw materials; to give us some reason for this proposal of each farm; to increase the associated aid in a way that makes up a large part of the direct aid; to set up measures to respond more rapidly to the price fluctuations of agricultural products; to replace the agricultural products; to replace the agricultural sector, to replace the agricultural sector's even pay them in terms. Of course, this explained so by the counsellor in the last plenary session, the truth is that even sounds good, but we, from En Marea, believe that there are some things left to point out. The first is that we see that it is necessary to define who will be the perceiver of the CAP. We have to establish what a genuine farmer is going to be — not yet defined —. In order to collect the aid, it must be required that at least 50% of the income comes from agricultural activity, and that person who receives these funds who spend more than 50% of his working time on agriculture, unless we finally fix population in rural areas and we have empty villages. The historic rights we, from En Marea, understand that they must disappear. We cannot ignore the importance of the CAP for Galicia, Galicia is an eminently rural people, it is a livestock people, agrarian people, who have to take this opportunity to attract and fix the population, to involve these key sectors in our country and to improve the social and economic fabric that enables them to develop these activities without having to use emigration, ensuring an equitable standard of living for farmers and farmers, and also ensuring prices for food for consumers. It is a great opportunity for Galicia to ensure that by fulfilling the nine objectives set out in the CAP it will contribute to improving these strategic sectors. Yesterday the deadline for tabling amendments to the Regulation and to the reports of the European Parliament on the common agricultural policy was ended. We have entities like the Via Campesina that are developing a campaign across Europe to insist on the need to define a sustainable agricultural model that constitutes genuine support in rural areas. The current CAP and draft of the following CAP to En Marea are not valid for you. The future CAP must guarantee fair income to farmers and farmers, equal to the rest of the professions, and respond to society's expectations. This implies the transition of all European farms to more sustainable models and to agricultural agro-ecology: preserving the environment, preserving the climate and ensuring quality food for all citizens, including the most disadvantaged people. And for this we need public policies that regulate markets. None of this is happening today if you're talking about it in Galicia. In addition, we have some fundamental demands such as maintaining agricultural and farming activity oriented towards food production in all rural territories and giving priority to peasant youth. But not only during the early years of youth but also in the distribution of aid and the reservation of payment rights. We must set a mandatory top of direct payments — as you propose — but we can give it back and lower a little, perhaps, that amount that you announced from 100,000 euros and with a 50% deduction of labour costs on a real contract, and with a maximum limitation to the number of workers or workers. These programmes should also be limited to these aids: to grant compulsory direct payment to small-scale farmers and farmers from a minimum aid value, to maintain the current level of aid coupled and not to accept that crops intended for agro-fuels benefit from such aid. Those farms based on agro-ecological models must be recognized within the ecosystems, and the latter must be an aid and support to systems and models for climate and the environment. And not specific practical help within an intensive model and agricultural system. Since En Marea we have strongly rejected compulsory funding for agricultural insurance to deal with market crises, agri-insurance. Risk management must be understood by public policies, such as — as we have said before — regulation of these markets. And all aid to farmers and farmers must be subject to the fulfilment of a series of common requirements relating to the reception rights of workers and agricultural workers, since this implies the inclusion of a social conditionality of the CAP which it does not have right now. Our demands are the demands of a large part of European society, why, because we ask to support a sustainable agricultural model that is fair and healthy for peasantry, citizenship and the environment in the context of Galicia. And, well, from there we ask — as we have already asked in the last plenary — that Galicia be strong, that it have a strategic plan of its own towards the next period of the CAP. For my part, nothing more and thank you very much.

Article 93 of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council 2018/2016, the architecture of the strategic plans of the CAP, provides that each Member State shall establish a single strategic plan of the CAP for all its territory. However, it also opens the door for the elements of the CAP's strategic plan to be established at a regional level. Article 94(3) states: "Each Member State shall establish an association with the competent local and regional authorities and that association shall include at least some interlocutors such as the relevant public authorities, the economic and social partners, the relevant bodies representing civil society and, where appropriate, the bodies responsible for promoting social inclusion, fundamental rights and gender equality and non-discrimination". Member States shall ensure that such interlocutors participate in the elaboration of the Strategic Plan of the CAP. In addition, the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food in its document entitled The Common Agricultural Policy post 2020, a response from Spain of 12 November 2018 establishes, inter alia, that Spain is committed to a common strategic plan for preserving the role of autonomous communities in the second pillar. We understand that the strategic plan must be made in accordance with the constitutional division of competences in our country between the state and the autonomous communities. It defends the existence of a national strategic plan in whose collaboration the autonomous communities must play a key role both in the intervention of the first pier and, in particular, in the interventions of the second pier. The strategic plan must be an opportunity to improve synergies between the measures of the first and second pillars to achieve the common objectives of the CAP. The intervention of the first pillar must continue to have a national character, while those of the second pillar must have a regional vocation when designed by the autonomous communities, because they are the best aware of the reality and needs of their territories. The governance model of the strategic plan should incorporate the authorities of the autonomous management communities, while ensuring the necessary coordination at the state level. Ladies and gentlemen, the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council and the document of the Ministry of Agriculture The Common Agricultural Policy post 2020, a response from Spain is very concrete and precise about the content, processing and role of the different administrations. Each Member State shall establish a single strategic plan of the CAP for all its territory. And each Member State will provide an association with the competent local and regional authorities to promote consideration of its specific elements...
